# Aggregate contracts, handlers, guards and tests

This catalog records current source behavior. Selected replacement contracts and exact target signatures are indexed in [README.md](README.md); [implementation decisions](implementation-decisions.md) and [owner protocols](owner-protocols.md) supersede conditional split sketches below.

Generated from current source inventory at `b7a2bd02cc90c6734c94391399a55c0da1621521`. This is the implementation contract index for the 103 audited aggregate owners. The JSON companion is [`aggregate-contract-catalog.json`](aggregate-contract-catalog.json); exact C# field types and request transport routes are in [`command-event-schemas.md`](command-event-schemas.md).

## Reading the catalog

- “Commands” are typed request contracts that currently target the aggregate, with their registered handlers, authorization-scope markers, identity fields and full target set. `—` means the coverage scan found no registered command route; it is a gap to resolve, not an implied handler.
- “Emits” lists current event contracts whose emitter is the aggregate or its partial source file, including scoped shared-helper facts and migration-control facts. “Reduces” lists the full accepted/replay vocabulary. Process/migration facts and replay-only facts are identified separately.
- The guard and test lists define required behavior. Existing test paths are navigation; a path does not prove the scenario asserted. For every command mutation, the handler authorizes before retry lookup, binds the declared owner, checks exact accepted intent, and delegates atomic business invariants to the owner. Cross-owner writes use immutable exact-scope proofs.

## 1. AccessGrant

- **Identity:** Tenant+grantId; caller supplies stable grant identity. Issue compares terms/episode; Revoke exact retry conflicts. Portia expected stream position handles append OCC.
- **Invariant:** One stable grant identity has one immutable set of scope/principal/effective terms and, once revoked, can never be reissued.
- **Boundary:** retain. Splitting terms from revocation could permit reissue or activation with mismatched membership episode. No other grants need the same revision.
- **Current observations:** ACCESS-10 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Handler supplies validated proposal/current membershipEpisodeId value; aggregate reads no other owner. Separate member eligibility is admission and later authority checks.
  - State/frame guard: Scalar current terms, episode, revoked. At most two business facts per grant; lifecycle domain replay is bounded independently of number of grants.

    - Helper/coordination: `TenantManagerInvariant, MemberAuthorityCleanup`. Guards: Authorize before resolving exact retries; use the request authorization scope IClientRbacMutationRequest. ## 2. AccessPopulation

- **Identity:** tenant/access-populations/populationId; Open handler uses stable request identity. Open compares initial creation terms. Record uses population Revision; Classify uses principal history count but both append to the same physical stream. Mutation retry receipts are absent for Record/Classify.
- **Invariant:** An accepted population is an immutable, internally valid snapshot; each accepted principal classification has a contiguous decision count and valid identity shape.
- **Boundary:** split. Separating mutable draft facts from acceptance without a fence permits accepting a different set than the validated snapshot. Principal classification decisions can be separate from the immutable population using exact accepted-population and principal proofs; nothing requires classifications of unrelated principals to serialize.
- **Current observations:** OPS-07 (pending), OPS-15 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Handler checks Person/ServiceIdentity existence and application visibility at admission; aggregate receives IDs and immutable facts, no live aggregate argument.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Separating mutable draft facts from acceptance without a fence permits accepting a different set than the validated snapshot. Principal classification decisions can be separate from the immutable population using exact accepted-population and principal proofs; nothing requires classifications of unrelated principals to serialize.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "Whole current principal/entitlement/group/assignment graph plus every classification decision of every principal.", "lifetimeReplay": "Draft replacements, acceptance and repeated classifications accumulate forever in one population stream; MaximumFacts bounds current graph, not replacements or classification history."}

## 3. AccessReviewCampaign

- **Identity:** tenant/access-review-campaigns/context.RequestId; itemId derives from campaign+population+subject+entitlement. Revision shared across all commands. Reassignment has exact-intent replay; Decide only matches decisionId and returns previous decisions regardless of changed intent.
- **Invariant:** Completion is admitted only after every frozen review item is decided and each required remediation is verified or covered by a live exception; reviewer and remediation owner stay distinct per item.
- **Boundary:** split. A naive item split breaks the atomic completion gate: a new or changed decision can invalidate remediation while completion uses an old summary. Keep a campaign completion coordinator with sealed item generations/completion proofs, while independent review/remediation item lifecycles move to their own streams.
- **Current observations:** OPS-07 (pending), OPS-11 (pending), OPS-16 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Decide receives live SeparationOfDutiesWaiver and calls Allows. Launch handler supplies frozen snapshots/value items from other owners, which is admission and does not require live aggregate reads inside campaign.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: A naive item split breaks the atomic completion gate: a new or changed decision can invalidate remediation while completion uses an old summary. Keep a campaign completion coordinator with sealed item generations/completion proofs, while independent review/remediation item lifecycles move to their own streams.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "All frozen items and ordering, per-item complete decision/change/reassignment histories, verification/exception and campaign-wide retry map.", "lifetimeReplay": "Frozen item count may be large; repeated decisions/provider changes/reassignments grow command state and replay without bound before completion."}
- **Commands and handlers:**

Owner-specific cross-boundary rule: Decide receives live SeparationOfDutiesWaiver and calls Allows. Launch handler supplies frozen snapshots/value items from other owners, which is admission and does not require live aggregate reads inside campaign.

## 4. AccessReviewSystemLedger

- **Identity:** tenant/access-review-systems/systemInstanceId; all IDs are child identities. Every mutation checks one ledger Revision. Propose only compares proposer for reused expectationId; exceptions return reused IDs without intent comparison.
- **Invariant:** Each expectation can be independently approved by someone other than its proposer unless exactly waived; an approved successor changes the effective expectation line. Exceptions require an approved expectation and future expiry.
- **Boundary:** split. Unrelated expectations and missing-population exceptions have no joint atomic rule. Split expectation lineage by business rule identity; coordinate successor admission only with its own predecessor and keep system coverage as a projection. Exceptions are their own decisions with their own IDs.
- **Current observations:** OPS-07 (pending), OPS-11 (pending), OPS-16 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Approve takes live SeparationOfDutiesWaiver, invokes Allows. Other system/existence facts are handler admission values.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Unrelated expectations and missing-population exceptions have no joint atomic rule. Split expectation lineage by business rule identity; coordinate successor admission only with its own predecessor and keep system coverage as a projection. Exceptions are their own decisions with their own IDs.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "All expectation versions, proposer map, every rule exception and every missing-population exception.", "lifetimeReplay": "Every expectation, supersession and expiring exception remains retained for system lifetime; no lifetime bound."}
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `ApproveAccessExpectation` → ApproveAccessExpectationHandler; targets `AccessReviewSystemLedger`; scope `IRequest<AccessExpectationView>, IAccessReviewMutationRequest`; authorizer `[AccessReviewAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessReviews/AccessReviewAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, SystemInstanceId, ExpectationId, ExpectedLedgerRevision`.

## 5. AdvisorHandoff

- **Identity:** Tenant / advisor-handoffs / handoffId; feedbackId/correctionId supplied; create compares current selection not original after selection changes; responses have no command/response identity and always append; end/readiness/selection exact retries fail state/revision. Pinned Portia uses full physical stream replay and expected physical position; no durable request dedup is supplied.
- **Invariant:** Feedback admission must occur while this handoff is ready and unrevoked/closed, against an exactly selected source version. Feedback corrections/responses preserve original contribution; selection change makes feedback historical.
- **Boundary:** split. Naively splitting feedback loses atomic admission-versus-revocation gate. Retain compact handoff admission authority; individual feedback and its correction/response lifecycle can live separately under exact admission proof. No write rule spans two feedback items.
- **Current observations:** ROOTASSURE-01 (pending), ROOTASSURE-04 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Exact source-reference values only; handlers establish actor/resource authority. No public method takes a live aggregate.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Naively splitting feedback loses atomic admission-versus-revocation gate. Retain compact handoff admission authority; individual feedback and its correction/response lifecycle can live separately under exact admission proof. No write rule spans two feedback items.
  - State/frame guard: Current selection max100, but all feedback with all correction/response lists retained forever and read from aggregate. Every feedback operation extends lifetime replay; no lifecycle cap.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `CloseAdvisorHandoff` → CloseAdvisorHandoffHandler; targets `AdvisorHandoff`; scope `IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, HandoffId, Rationale`.

## 6. ApplicationImportLedger

- **Identity:** Source identity length-encodes source key/namespace with tenant. Batch/submission ids stable. Acceptance/correlation check prior intent, but business and technical histories are retained as dedup maps.
- **Invariant:** One source has at most one acceptance in progress; source record identities cannot be rebound, and source acceptance becomes visible only when every sealed target effect is durable at its exact expected revision.
- **Boundary:** split. Source-wide epoch/admission gate is real, but all execution plans and per-record claims need not share it forever. Narrow coordinator, recoverable ordered reservations and complete seal must preserve original atomic acceptance; per-target commands alone lose it.
- **Current observations:** ROOT-02 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Methods receive live ImportBatch and DeclaredApplication dictionaries for admission, commit verification and retirement. Multi-target application effects follow plan order rather than canonical target hold order. A seal-triggered execution reactor exists, but receiver conflict/recovery remains material.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Source-wide epoch/admission gate is real, but all execution plans and per-record claims need not share it forever. Narrow coordinator, recoverable ordered reservations and complete seal must preserve original atomic acceptance; per-target commands alone lose it.
  - State/frame guard: All cancellations, correlations, committed revisions, frozen plan rows, failures and accepted source bindings across every batch retained.200 effects bounds one batch, not lifetime.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `AcceptApplicationImport` → AcceptApplicationImportHandler; targets `ApplicationImportLedger`; scope `IApplicationInventoryWriteRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ApplicationInventoryAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Applications/ApplicationInventoryAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, BatchId, ExpectedBatchRevision`.

## 7. ArtifactDisclosure

- **Identity:** Tenant / artifact-disclosures / DisclosureId; changed initial intent conflicts; logical expectedRevision on delivery/revoke; approval/delivery compare server timestamps for retries. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** One disclosure binds immutable artifact content, handling-policy revision, recipient and purpose; delivery requires that approved identity and revocation is final.
- **Boundary:** retain. Separating approval from delivery without a one-use approval authorization could deliver changed content or a different recipient. These three monotonic states fit together.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-10 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Source snapshot is a value; source and authority are read/fenced by handlers before admission; aggregate does not hydrate another owner.
  - State/frame guard: One frozen source and at most three lifecycle states; no child roster and intrinsically finite replay.
- **Commands and handlers:**

  - `RevokeArtifactDisclosure` → RevokeArtifactDisclosureHandler; targets `ArtifactDisclosure`; scope `IArtifactDisclosureRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ArtifactDisclosureAccess.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Retention/ArtifactDisclosureAccess.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, DisclosureId, ArtifactId, ExpectedRevision, Rationale`.

  - Test exact retry after later edits/removal/reinstatement; same identity with changed actor, kind, intent or expected revision conflicts without appending.
  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: Separating approval from delivery without a one-use approval authorization could deliver changed content or a different recipient. These three monotonic states fit together.
  - Proof/authorization test: Source snapshot is a value; source and authority are read/fenced by handlers before admission; aggregate does not hydrate another owner.

## 8. ArtifactDisposition

- **Identity:** Tenant / artifact-dispositions / dispositionId; explicit revision; result handler binds execution-event ID and attempt number; approval retry includes ApprovedAt. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** Only an approved exact source with elapsed retention/no active holds or reliance may start execution; one outstanding attempt; disposed result is terminal and yields tombstone.
- **Boundary:** process. Approval and execution need an explicit durable authorization token and one outstanding executor claim if separated; the lifetime attempt history itself is unnecessary to that rule.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-15 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Eligibility is a value snapshot. Approval/execution routes are not registered in current inventory; result-only worker verifies exact causation. No claim that a live destruction race is presently reachable.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Approval and execution need an explicit durable authorization token and one outstanding executor claim if separated; the lifetime attempt history itself is unnecessary to that rule.
  - State/frame guard: Retains all attempts and full eligibility/tombstone in every state; MaximumAttempts=16 eventually forbids further recovery after transient failures.

- **Additional replay facts:** none.

  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: Approval and execution need an explicit durable authorization token and one outstanding executor claim if separated; the lifetime attempt history itself is unnecessary to that rule.

  - Growth test: Single hold, issuance and release only; at most three facts, independent of engagement roster.

## 10. ArtifactRetention

- **Identity:** UUIDv5(tenant, sourceKind/sourceId), with shared logical revision; hold IDs detect changed intent; basis/hold retries compare recorded actor/reason. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** One exact content identity has one supported seven-year retention basis; each legal hold may release once after placement. No disposal is authorized here.
- **Boundary:** split. No current command decides across two legal holds; Assess only counts them and always reports additional blockers. Individual legal holds can move to their own identities. A future no-active-hold disposal gate must be coordinated explicitly.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-12 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Source snapshot is passed by value and exact content checked. Assess never grants disposal: it adds unavailable authority/reliance/approval/storage blockers.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: No current command decides across two legal holds; Assess only counts them and always reports additional blockers. Individual legal holds can move to their own identities. A future no-active-hold disposal gate must be coordinated explicitly.
  - State/frame guard: Dictionary keeps every legal hold including released holds forever. Every hold placement/release adds to one source lifetime replay. Basis/source is constant size.

## 11. AuditObservation

- **Identity:** Tenant / audit-observations / observationId; program checked inside stream; mutation requires exact nonempty command selector; replay retains only selected command fact and compares intent before revision. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** Received auditor source corrections preserve examination/snapshot and exact source lineage; response/link commands must name current source version, and each selected command identity has immutable intent.
- **Boundary:** retain. A correction racing a response/link would lose the current-source-version gate if moved without source-owner admission authorization. Retaining these current facts is defensible; independent response/link ownership is a close alternative if historical version proofs suffice.
- **Current observations:** none
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Only source-reference values; no live aggregate arguments. No public command handler currently registered, pending source authorization.
  - State/frame guard: Keeps current received version, latest response/link and one selected fact; payload bound per fact. Lifetime replay still grows with corrections/responses/links, all on one observation.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - No command or internal reaction currently maps to this owner in the coverage inventory. Add an authorized owner request or classify this as a read-only/process/replay-only owner.
- **Events emitted:** `AuditObservationCorrected`, `AuditObservationFindingLinked`, `AuditObservationManagementResponseRecorded`, `AuditObservationRecorded`
- **Additional replay facts:** none.
- **Tests to lock the boundary:**
  - Given the owner invariant (“Received auditor source corrections preserve examination/snapshot and exact source lineage; response/link commands must name current source version, and each selected command identity has immutable intent.”), test allowed and rejected transitions, asserting rejected commands append no domain fact.
  - Test aggregate rehydration/replay after every accepted transition and after a later edit or lifecycle episode.
  - Test exact retry after later edits/removal/reinstatement; same identity with changed actor, kind, intent or expected revision conflicts without appending.
  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: A correction racing a response/link would lose the current-source-version gate if moved without source-owner admission authorization. Retaining these current facts is defensible; independent response/link ownership is a close alternative if historical version proofs suffice.
  - Proof/authorization test: Only source-reference values; no live aggregate arguments. No public command handler currently registered, pending source authorization.
  - Growth test: Keeps current received version, latest response/link and one selected fact; payload bound per fact. Lifetime replay still grows with corrections/responses/links, all on one observation.

## 13. AuditPackage

- **Identity:** Tenant / audit-packages / packageId; child IDs derived from manifest version and key/recipient; package expectedRevision; create retry includes generated timestamp and only works while currentVersion=1. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** A delivery must use exact selected manifest and successful archive, passed/current validation or independent complete overrides, and approval for recipient; delivered version content cannot change.
- **Boundary:** split. Those authorization gates require a compact coordinator/one-use delivery authorization after split. Build attempts, deliveries to different recipients and prior immutable versions need not live as parent state.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-02 (pending), ASSURE-10 (pending), CORE-01 (complete)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Handlers resolve/fence source values. Aggregate accepts content/output/findings values and never reads live other owners.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Those authorization gates require a compact coordinator/one-use delivery authorization after split. Build attempts, deliveries to different recipients and prior immutable versions need not live as parent state.
  - State/frame guard: Every immutable version, selected source manifest, all builds/validations/overrides/disclosures/deliveries copied into each event. Caps of64 builds,64 validations,64 approvals,128 deliveries; unbounded versions can hit 512KiB full-state bound.

## 14. AuditPopulation

- **Identity:** Tenant / audit-populations / populationId (Define handler uses DefinitionId); definition/source/row/export identities checked; one shared expectedRevision; several mutators check revision before retry. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** Freeze must bind exact definition, source inventories and complete per-category row reconciliation; frozen content is immutable and incomplete reconciliation stays sample-ineligible.
- **Boundary:** narrow. Keep current complete row/category/source reconciliation and authoritative freeze seal together. Immutable row/version content may use chunked capsules and history projections; independent export attempts need their own process/identity. No per-row projected counts may decide freeze.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-04 (pending), ASSURE-10 (pending), ASSURE-11 (pending), CORE-01 (complete)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Define accepts a TypeIIPeriodView value and checks immutable close snapshot; handlers hydrate authoritative period. No live aggregate reference.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Keep current complete row/category/source reconciliation and authoritative freeze seal together. Immutable row/version content may use chunked capsules and history projections; independent export attempts need their own process/identity. No per-row projected counts may decide freeze.
  - State/frame guard: Current rows/sources plus every frozen copy, definition version, amendment and export live in each State. 64 versions and256 exports cap lifetime;512KiB complete-state bound blocks normal growth sooner for rich rows.

Guards: Authorize before resolving exact retries; use the request authorization scope IRequest<AuditPopulationExportReceipt>, IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest. Validate tenant/program and typed owner identity against the target stream before loading or mutating it. For an accepted operation identity, compare owner, lifecycle episode, original actor, canonical authored intent and original expected revision; exact retry returns the original result without appending. For a new operation, enforce expected revision/physical append OCC. If a repeated mutation has no stable typed identity or revision, add it before enabling retries. For every other owner, pass a server-created immutable proof with source identity, event/position, revision, digest and required facts; do not pass a live aggregate as authority. Owner-specific cross-boundary rule: Define accepts a TypeIIPeriodView value and checks immutable close snapshot; handlers hydrate authoritative period. No live aggregate reference.

## 15. AuditorRequest

- **Identity:** Tenant / auditor-requests / requestId; response/selection IDs supplied, comments minted random in aggregate with no command key; many handlers pass current aggregate.Revision automatically; creation compares generated timestamp. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** A response must be independently reviewed if required before exact delivery; request resolution requires at least one response and accepted external disposition for every admitted response.
- **Boundary:** split. Splitting all responses without registration/sealing loses the all-responses-accepted rule. Comment and sample selection lifecycles require no request-member atomic state; response coordinator can retain admission/count/closure proofs.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-03 (pending), ASSURE-09 (pending), ASSURE-10 (pending), ASSURE-11 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Evidence/population/member checks occur in handlers; aggregate accepts values only. Open request admission must become explicit source-owner authorization if children split.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Splitting all responses without registration/sealing loses the all-responses-accepted rule. Comment and sample selection lifecycles require no request-member atomic state; response coordinator can retain admission/count/closure proofs.
  - State/frame guard: All terms/history/comments/responses/all response versions/deliveries/dispositions/sample selections kept and copied each event; caps256 comments,64 responses,16 versions,64 selections,512 total history and48KiB state bound.

## 16. ClientExport

- **Identity:** Tenant / client-exports / exportId; attemptId and sequence checked, final delivery immutable and exact; result handler binds requested event; expected revision. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** Final export delivery requires archive counts matching frozen included/withheld inventory and a successful exact-manifest delivery for each required attest package.
- **Boundary:** process. The all-required-deliveries gate must stay in a compact export completion coordinator if executor/attest delivery lifecycles split; source content can be immutable chunks/proof.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-15 (pending), CORE-01 (complete)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Content/source inventory values supplied by owning workflow; no aggregate reads.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: The all-required-deliveries gate must stay in a compact export completion coordinator if executor/attest delivery lifecycles split; source content can be immutable chunks/proof.
  - State/frame guard: Full frozen source inventory, all build attempts and per-package delivery attempts copied per event.32 build attempts and32 delivery attempts per required package;512KiB state ceiling; no arbitrary lifetime cap justified by business rule.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `RecordClientExportBuildResult` → RecordClientExportBuildResultHandler; targets `ClientExport`; scope `feature authorizer not explicit in interface`; authorizer `[RecordClientExportBuildResultAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Exports/RecordClientExportBuildResultAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ExportId, ExpectedRevision, BuildRequestEventId, Result, CompletedAt`.
  - `RecordClientExportDelivery` → RecordClientExportDeliveryHandler; targets `ClientExport`; scope `IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ExportId, ExpectedRevision, Recipient, DeliveredAt, DeliveryReference`.
- **Events emitted:** `ClientExportAttestPackageDeliveryRecorded`, `ClientExportBuildFailed`, `ClientExportBuilt`, `ClientExportDelivered`, `ClientExportRequested`
- **Additional replay facts:** none.
- **Tests to lock the boundary:**
  - Given the owner invariant (“Final export delivery requires archive counts matching frozen included/withheld inventory and a successful exact-manifest delivery for each required attest package.”), test allowed and rejected transitions, asserting rejected commands append no domain fact.
  - Test aggregate rehydration/replay after every accepted transition and after a later edit or lifecycle episode.
  - Test exact retry after later edits/removal/reinstatement; same identity with changed actor, kind, intent or expected revision conflicts without appending.
  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: The all-required-deliveries gate must stay in a compact export completion coordinator if executor/attest delivery lifecycles split; source content can be immutable chunks/proof.

## 17. ClientService

- **Identity:** Tenant / client-services / serviceId, Create handler uses context.RequestId; initial content preserved across edits; revision/retirement checked before replay comparisons on mutators. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** Once retired a service cannot be revised, and its program identity cannot be rebound; create identity is immutable.
- **Boundary:** retain. No independent child lifecycle is retained. Lifecycle and changes to this service belong together.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-11 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Program existence checked in handler at admission; service does not read a program root.
  - State/frame guard: Constant current lifecycle and initial-create content; lifetime replay grows with edits to the same service, not other services. No artificial cap.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `CreateClientService` → CreateClientServiceHandler; targets `ClientService`; scope `IRequest<ClientServiceRegistration>, IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, Name, Purpose, OwnerReference`.

  - `ReviseClientService` → ReviseClientServiceHandler; targets `ClientService`; scope `IClientServiceProgramResourceRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ServiceId, ExpectedRevision, Name, Purpose, OwnerReference`.
- **Events emitted:** `ClientServiceCreated`, `ClientServiceRetired`, `ClientServiceRevised`
- **Additional replay facts:** none.
- **Tests to lock the boundary:**
  - Given the owner invariant (“Once retired a service cannot be revised, and its program identity cannot be rebound; create identity is immutable.”), test allowed and rejected transitions, asserting rejected commands append no domain fact.
  - Test aggregate rehydration/replay after every accepted transition and after a later edit or lifecycle episode.
  - Test exact retry after later edits/removal/reinstatement; same identity with changed actor, kind, intent or expected revision conflicts without appending.
  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: No independent child lifecycle is retained. Lifecycle and changes to this service belong together.
  - Proof/authorization test: Program existence checked in handler at admission; service does not read a program root.
  - Growth test: Constant current lifecycle and initial-create content; lifetime replay grows with edits to the same service, not other services. No artificial cap.

## 18. CommitmentDraft

- **Identity:** UUIDv5 tenant+program+normalizedKind/identifier; explicit createRequestId retained and compares complete initial normalized terms. Revision expected; review/approval DecisionId=RequestId but review lacks retry comparison. Retirement compares generated timestamp so handler retry fails. Physical append OCC.
- **Invariant:** One tenant/program/kind/identifier has immutable identity/source creation, revisioned authored content, exact verified-source accepted review and independent approval that publishes strictly later effective versions; retired commitment remains terminal.
- **Boundary:** narrow. Current pending authors, accepted review and current-revision responsibility conflicts must remain atomically coherent. Past effective version list/history query and old scope sets can leave current command state. Provider/service owners can be checked at admission using values.
- **Current observations:** ROOTACCESS-01 (pending), ROOTACCESS-02 (pending), ROOTACCESS-04 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Live SeparationOfDutiesWaiver values accepted and Allows invoked inside owner/helper. Provider/service source hydration occurs in handler and passes IDs/values, which is admission rather than direct aggregate reading inside root.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Current pending authors, accepted review and current-revision responsibility conflicts must remain atomically coherent. Past effective version list/history query and old scope sets can leave current command state. Provider/service owners can be checked at admission using values.
  - State/frame guard: Immutable creation values, latest source, pending author set, current accepted review, all version/effective tuples and responsibility sets/decision lists by every revision, terminal retirement fields. Typed JSON context48KiB event checks are correct and unaffected by CORE-01. No lifetime cap; stream replay and histories grow every edit/decision.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `ApproveCommitmentDraft` → ApproveCommitmentDraftHandler; targets `CommitmentDraft`; scope `IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, DraftId, ExpectedRevision, AcceptedReviewDecisionId, EffectiveFrom, Rationale, ImpactDigest`.
  - `AssignResponsibility` → AssignResponsibilityHandler; targets `CommitmentDraft, ControlDraft, SystemBoundary`; scope `IRbacManagementRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [RbacManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/RbacManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, MemberUserId, Type, RecordType, RecordId, VersionId, ScopeRevision, EffectiveFrom, SeparationOfDutiesWaiverIds, Scope`.
  - `CreateCommitmentDraft` → CreateCommitmentDraftHandler; targets `CommitmentDraft`; scope `IRequest<CommitmentDraftRegistration>, IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, ServiceId, Kind, Identifier, Statement, Context, SourceReference`.
  - `RetireCommitment` → RetireCommitmentHandler; targets `CommitmentDraft`; scope `IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, CommitmentId, ExpectedRevision, EffectiveOn, Rationale`.

## 19. ComplianceIncident

- **Identity:** Per incident id is a valid stable creation identity. SameInitial/revision/assessment helpers compare server RecordedAt; fresh handler clock makes an exact client retry conflict or append. No selected RequestId guard.
- **Invariant:** One incident version may be assessed once; current-version independent description decision and required follow-up eligibility gate advancement; incident resolution requires every admitted follow-up work item completed.
- **Boundary:** narrow. Keep current version, assessment and eligibility/independence gate atomic. Historical versions/decisions are immutable histories; do not split current gate simply by entity nouns. Required work selection can be sealed exact-version content with authority proofs.
- **Current observations:** ROOT-06 (pending), ROOT-07 (pending), CORE-01 (complete), ROOT-08 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Cross-owner work completion must arrive as authoritative immutable proof, not caller assertion or projection. For current descriptions, pass exact-version value proof and preserve owner independence.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Keep current version, assessment and eligibility/independence gate atomic. Historical versions/decisions are immutable histories; do not split current gate simply by entity nouns. Required work selection can be sealed exact-version content with authority proofs.
  - State/frame guard: Full version/assessment/decision chronology is embedded in every event and _current; lifetime32 versions is an invented growth limit. Physical replay and cumulative serialization grow even when current presentation is small.
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `AssessComplianceIncident` → AssessComplianceIncidentHandler; targets `ComplianceIncident`; scope `IRestrictedComplianceRecordMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [RestrictedComplianceRecordMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Changes/RestrictedComplianceRecordMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, IncidentId, ExpectedRevision, Content`.

  - `RecordComplianceIncidentResolution` → RecordComplianceIncidentResolutionHandler; targets `ComplianceIncident`; scope `IRestrictedComplianceRecordMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [RestrictedComplianceRecordMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Changes/RestrictedComplianceRecordMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, IncidentId, ExpectedRevision, Content`.

- **Implementation amendment (S10, ROOT-08):** The specification requires follow-up work completion to arrive as an authoritative immutable proof but names no single owner fact, because a work item is derived from its source workflow and `WorkItemAssignment` owns only assignment and escalation history. The minimal authoritative fact chosen is the committed `CorrectiveActionCompletedV2` of the corrective action on its finding stream: the work item id of a corrective action is the deterministic `WorkCandidate.IdFor(actionId, "corrective_action")`. `RecordComplianceIncidentResolution` therefore carries `NativeFactReference`s instead of work ids; the handler resolves each one through the source-fact resolver into a `SourceFactProof<CorrectiveActionCompletedV2>`, requires the same tenant and program and exactly one fact per assessed follow-up work item, and the incident retains the proofs in its resolution. A follow-up kind with no completion fact in this table cannot be resolved until its owning workflow publishes one and `FollowUpCompletionVerifier` maps it.

## 20. ComplianceProgram

- **Identity:** Tenant / programs / programId, creation uses context.RequestId; original create content remembered; revision for mutations; criteria selection has narrow latest retry special case. Portia appends against the hydrated physical stream position; a logical revision does not create another physical boundary.
- **Invariant:** Program target dates must form a coherent chronological plan; one program identity has immutable creation intent. Criteria selection separately chooses current edition.
- **Boundary:** narrow. Remove historical criteria-selection collection from command state while preserving authoritative as-of selection proofs. Keep coherent program date plan together. A separate current criteria-selection owner is a possible targeted split, not yet a required new aggregate. Splitting individual plan dates would lose chronological validation. Dropping criteria history without replacing authoritative temporal lookup would lose exact baseline/readiness source semantics. A stale projection cannot substitute for the contributor current-source check. Splitting current edition from program plan causes no observed write-rule loss, but also needs no invented new business lifecycle.
- **Current observations:** ASSURE-16 (pending), ASSURE-11 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Preserve authoritative temporal criteria-selection proof at readiness/baseline admission. Existing contributors recheck exact source selection; a lagging history projection cannot replace this gate. Keep creation intent and coherent plan validation. Move temporal selection reads to a dedicated authoritative history/proof reader; retain only current edition/current revision where command decisions need them. If separating current edition writes, use a stable program-scoped selection owner rather than a new aggregate per selection history entry.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Remove historical criteria-selection collection from command state while preserving authoritative as-of selection proofs. Keep coherent program date plan together. A separate current criteria-selection owner is a possible targeted split, not yet a required new aggregate. Splitting individual plan dates would lose chronological validation. Dropping criteria history without replacing authoritative temporal lookup would lose exact baseline/readiness source semantics. A stale projection cannot substitute for the contributor current-source check. Splitting current edition from program plan causes no observed write-rule loss, but also needs no invented new business lifecycle.
  - State/frame guard: Constant plan-create identity/current edition plus ever-growing criteria-edition history retained solely for as-of reads. Replay also grows with program edits/selections.

## 21. ControlCriterionMappingLedger

- **Identity:** Stream currently tenant/control-criterion-mappings/programId. MappingIdFor derives program+control+edition+criterion. Logical Revision is per mapping but physical OCC is program-wide. Pending proposal retry compares terms; Review/Retire lack durable retry handling.
- **Invariant:** For a mapping tuple (control, edition, criterion), only one pending proposal and one accepted version exist; acceptance independently reviews and supersedes the previous accepted version.
- **Boundary:** split. Nothing in Propose/Review/Retire compares different mapping tuples. Derived MappingId already enforces uniqueness, so separate tuple streams preserve invariant; program coverage/listing is projection work.
- **Current observations:** OPS-01 (pending), OPS-11 (pending), OPS-15 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.

  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Handler validates selected edition and exact approved Control value at admission; ledger itself receives IDs. Review receives live waiver and calls Allows.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Nothing in Propose/Review/Retire compares different mapping tuples. Derived MappingId already enforces uniqueness, so separate tuple streams preserve invariant; program coverage/listing is projection work.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "All program mappings, ordering and full proposed/reviewed/retired version histories.", "lifetimeReplay": "Program-wide stream and dictionary grow with all tuples and all revisions indefinitely."}

## 22. ControlDraft

- **Identity:** tenant/controls/controlId; controlId derives tenant+program+normalized identifier. Create stores creation requestId+initial content and distinguishes changed intent. Later Review/Approve/Retire/periodic commands do not generally retain request intent; content revision differs from physical stream revision.
- **Invariant:** Only the exact current draft or retirement can be independently reviewed then activated, and accepted successor/retirement changes one control version line without overlapping effective intervals. Responsibilities cannot create unwaived conflicts with decisions already made on the exact revision.
- **Boundary:** narrow. Splitting draft review/approval from the current target or conflicting responsibilities would break the exact accepted review and historical SoD invariant. Keep that narrow decision context atomic; approved versions, old scopes, and full decision/retirement histories can be read-side records.
- **Current observations:** OPS-11 (pending), OPS-13 (pending), OPS-15 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.

## 23. ControlEvaluationLedger

- **Identity:** tenant/control-evaluations/programId; evaluationId is stable context.RequestId from handler; per-evaluation logical Revision, program-wide physical OCC. Start compares frozen procedure; later review replay only checks decisionId and mutations generally lack retry receipts.
- **Invariant:** Within one evaluation round: freeze exact procedure/control version, accept only coherent assertion conclusions, require minor deviation disposition, and independently review submitted work; material deviations require a retest lineage.
- **Boundary:** split. Different evaluations do not share a completion rule. Split per evaluation; original accepted material-deviation proof admits a retest, and a projection shows original/retest roster. Steps stay together because conclusions depend atomically on the complete current procedure result set.
- **Current observations:** OPS-02 (pending), OPS-11 (pending), OPS-15 (pending), OPS-16 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Immutable plan/procedure passed by value from handler. DisposeDeviation and Review receive live waiver and invoke Allows.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Different evaluations do not share a completion rule. Split per evaluation; original accepted material-deviation proof admits a retest, and a projection shows original/retest roster. Steps stay together because conclusions depend atomically on the complete current procedure result set.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "Every evaluation with frozen steps, latest results and complete deviations, submission snapshots and review history.", "lifetimeReplay": "One program accumulates every evaluation/retest and all rounds; each submission duplicates full current step/deviation results."}

Validate tenant/program and typed owner identity against the target stream before loading or mutating it. For an accepted operation identity, compare owner, lifecycle episode, original actor, canonical authored intent and original expected revision; exact retry returns the original result without appending. ## 24. ControlEvaluationPlan

- **Identity:** tenant/control-evaluation-plans/controlId; stable planVersionId passed from request; Define compares complete procedure/author/current version data on identity reuse and checks expectedVersion.
- **Invariant:** A plan version has a stable identity and immutable procedure; only a successor to the current plan version can be appended and exact duplicate identity must have the same content.
- **Boundary:** narrow. The procedure steps belong together: validation and eventual evaluation operate over one exact ordered procedure snapshot. Old immutable plan versions do not need to stay in active command state; keep current version/identity admission while history is a projection.
- **Current observations:** OPS-13 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Handler supplies exact control version identity; aggregate receives no live aggregate and validates same-control references.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: The procedure steps belong together: validation and eventual evaluation operate over one exact ordered procedure snapshot. Old immutable plan versions do not need to stay in active command state; keep current version/identity admission while history is a projection.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "All immutable procedure versions, each with full step and inspected-item payload.", "lifetimeReplay": "Current step/item limits bound one version; version count and replay history grow with every redefinition."}
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `DefineControlEvaluationPlan` → DefineControlEvaluationPlanHandler; targets `ControlEvaluationPlan`; scope `IRequest<ControlEvaluationPlanVersionView>, IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, ControlId, ControlVersionId, ExpectedVersion, Objective, TesterIndependenceRequired`.
- **Events emitted:** `ControlEvaluationPlanVersionDefined`
- **Additional replay facts:** none.
- **Tests to lock the boundary:**
  - Given the owner invariant (“A plan version has a stable identity and immutable procedure; only a successor to the current plan version can be appended and exact duplicate identity must have the same content.”), test allowed and rejected transitions, asserting rejected commands append no domain fact.
  - Test aggregate rehydration/replay after every accepted transition and after a later edit or lifecycle episode.
  - Test exact retry after later edits/removal/reinstatement; same identity with changed actor, kind, intent or expected revision conflicts without appending.
  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: The procedure steps belong together: validation and eventual evaluation operate over one exact ordered procedure snapshot. Old immutable plan versions do not need to stay in active command state; keep current version/identity admission while history is a projection.
  - Proof/authorization test: Handler supplies exact control version identity; aggregate receives no live aggregate and validates same-control references.
  - Growth test: {"retained": "All immutable procedure versions, each with full step and inspected-item payload.", "lifetimeReplay": "Current step/item limits bound one version; version count and replay history grow with every redefinition."}

## 25. ControlOperationsLedger

- **Identity:** tenant/control-operations/programId; planVersionId uses request identity, recurring occurrenceId derives control+periodStart, ad hoc occurrenceId uses request identity. Per-line and per-occurrence logical revisions share program physical OCC. Several retry paths only check ID/proposer; review identity ignores changed content.
- **Invariant:** One control operating-plan line has independently approved non-overlapping effective versions; one occurrence can be attested/corrected and independently reviewed against its exact frozen plan and evidence requirements. Plan approval reassigns every open/returned occurrence to the approved owner.
- **Boundary:** split. Different controls and occurrences do not need one program stream. However a naive plan/occurrence split would lose atomic owner reassignment of open work. Use a per-control plan admission generation and recoverable canonical occurrence reassignment process, or keep only the small necessary ownership coordination; each occurrence owns its own attestation lifecycle.
- **Current observations:** OPS-03 (pending), OPS-11 (pending), OPS-15 (pending), OPS-16 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: ControlVersionView and versionUntil are values supplied from control owner at admission. Plan/occurrence review gets live waiver and calls Allows.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: Different controls and occurrences do not need one program stream. However a naive plan/occurrence split would lose atomic owner reassignment of open work. Use a per-control plan admission generation and recoverable canonical occurrence reassignment process, or keep only the small necessary ownership coordination; each occurrence owns its own attestation lifecycle.

## 26. CriteriaTextOverlayLedger

- **Identity:** tenant/criteria-text-overlays/editionId; per-entry identifier/revision and requestId are already explicit. Set retains full exact request intent and checks per-entry expectedRevision, but all entries share physical OCC.
- **Invariant:** One supplied text/license entry can change only from its exact previous revision; one request identity cannot establish two different overlay intents.
- **Boundary:** split. No rule compares different criterion identifiers in an edition. Split tenant+edition+identifier entry streams; preserve immutable revisions and per-request intent checks, with catalog/list projection.
- **Current observations:** OPS-05 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Catalog existence checked at handler admission; aggregate receives entry string/content value, no live aggregate.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: No rule compares different criterion identifiers in an edition. Split tenant+edition+identifier entry streams; preserve immutable revisions and per-request intent checks, with catalog/list projection.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "Latest full supplied text and license per identifier plus full content/expected revision tuple for every successful request.", "lifetimeReplay": "Dictionary entry count and request map grow for edition lifetime; 48KiB event bound controls one change only."}
- **Commands and handlers:**
  - `SetCriteriaTextOverlay` → SetCriteriaTextOverlayHandler; targets `CriteriaTextOverlayLedger`; scope `IRequest<CriteriaTextOverlayRegistration>, IProgramManagementRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, EditionId, Identifier, Content`.
- **Events emitted:** `CriteriaTextOverlayEntryRevised`
- **Additional replay facts:** none.
- **Tests to lock the boundary:**
  - Given the owner invariant (“One supplied text/license entry can change only from its exact previous revision; one request identity cannot establish two different overlay intents.”), test allowed and rejected transitions, asserting rejected commands append no domain fact.
  - Test aggregate rehydration/replay after every accepted transition and after a later edit or lifecycle episode.
  - Test exact retry after later edits/removal/reinstatement; same identity with changed actor, kind, intent or expected revision conflicts without appending.
  - Test expected-revision races: concurrent writers cannot both advance the same owner decision.
  - Test retained-state and serialized-event limits with combined maximum fields, attribution and Unicode; no lifetime cap may stand in for an owner split.
  - Boundary test: No rule compares different criterion identifiers in an edition. Split tenant+edition+identifier entry streams; preserve immutable revisions and per-request intent checks, with catalog/list projection.
  - Proof/authorization test: Catalog existence checked at handler admission; aggregate receives entry string/content value, no live aggregate.
  - Growth test: {"retained": "Latest full supplied text and license per identifier plus full content/expected revision tuple for every successful request.", "lifetimeReplay": "Dictionary entry count and request map grow for edition lifetime; 48KiB event bound controls one change only."}

## 27. CriterionApplicabilityLedger

- **Identity:** tenant/criterion-applicability/programId; DecisionIdFor derives program+edition+criterion. Logical revision belongs to one decision, physical append belongs to program. Pending proposal handles immediate exact retry only; review/withdraw have no committed replay path.
- **Invariant:** One edition+criterion applicability line has at most one pending proposal; only independent accepted review makes it not applicable, and withdrawal restores coverage while preserving prior fact.
- **Boundary:** split. No other criterion participates in Propose/Review/Withdraw. Split by derived DecisionId and make program criterion coverage/history a projection; no reservation across unrelated criteria required.
- **Current observations:** OPS-01 (pending), OPS-11 (pending), OPS-15 (pending)
- **Guards:**
  - Authenticate and authorize before retry resolution; scope the actor to this owner’s tenant/program and stream identity.
  - Use typed client operation identity for repeated mutations, then compare original actor, command kind, canonical intent, lifecycle episode and original expected revision.
  - Use owner-native facts for retry resolution; exact retries append nothing, changed intent conflicts, and new operations enforce expected revision.
  - Keep validation, uniqueness, status, approval and segregation rules in this aggregate’s atomic command boundary; reject without appending on failure.
  - Cross-owner guard: Selected edition and catalog criterion validated by handler, values only; Review reads live waiver via Allows.
  - Boundary/coordination guard: No other criterion participates in Propose/Review/Withdraw. Split by derived DecisionId and make program criterion coverage/history a projection; no reservation across unrelated criteria required.
  - State/frame guard: {"retained": "All program criterion decisions, complete versions, proposer dictionary per version and ordering.", "lifetimeReplay": "Both criteria population and all reproposal/review/withdraw versions accumulate without lifetime bound."}

  - `WithdrawCriterionNotApplicable` → WithdrawCriterionNotApplicableHandler; targets `CriterionApplicabilityLedger`; scope `IProgramScopedRequest, IClientManagementMutationRequest`; authorizer `[ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/AccessControl/ClientManagementMutationAuthorizer.cs), [ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs](../../../../src/Compliance.Core/Features/Programs/ProgramManagementAuthorizer.cs)`; typed contract fields `TenantId, ProgramId, DecisionId, ExpectedRevision, Rationale`.
